# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_celestrion-nexus-core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system simulates a small quantum communication layer. It manages entangled-pair resources, performs simplified measurements, sends and receives messages through those pairs, and represents transmitted messages as JSON-like dictionaries. The model is not a physics engine; it provides deterministic application-level behavior around a randomized communication primitive.

## 2. Functional Requirement

  An entangled pair is represented as a two-by-two numeric state generated from random amplitudes. The communication layer has a maximum number of stored pairs. Creating a pair adds it to the available
  pool until that limit is reached; attempts beyond the limit must throw `IllegalStateException`.

  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title nexus Model

class EntanglementManager {
    activePairs : List<DoubleArray2D>
    quantumComm : QuantumComm
    establishConnection() : EIntegerObject
    listActivePairs() : void
    receiveMessage(pairIndex : int) : String
    removePair(pairIndex : int) : void
    sendMessage(message : String, pairIndex : int) : void
}
class JsonHelper {
    escapeString(s : String) : String
    parseArray(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : List<EJavaObject>
    parseBool(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : boolean
    parseNull(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : EJavaObject
    parseNum(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : Number
    parseObj(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : EMap
    parseObject(json : String) : EMap
    parseStr(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : List<String>
    parseValue(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : EJavaObject
    serializeMap(map : EMap) : String
    skipWhitespace(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : void
    toJsonString(s : String) : String
    toJsonValue(value : EJavaObject) : String
}
class MessageProtocol {
    jsonHelper : JsonHelper
    addMetadata(key : String, messageDict : EMap, value : EJavaObject) : void
    formatMessage(recipient : String, message : String, metadata : EMap) : String
    getMetadata(messageDict : EMap, key : String) : EJavaObject
    parseMessage(rawMessage : String) : EMap
    removeMetadata(messageDict : EMap, key : String) : void
    validateMessage(messageDict : EMap) : boolean
}
class QuantumComm {
    entangledPairs : List<DoubleArray2D>
    maxPairs : int
    random : Random
    jsonHelper : JsonHelper
    createEntangledPair() : DoubleArray2D
    formatMessage(recipient : String, message : String) : String
    measureState(pairIndex : int) : int
    parseMessage(rawMessage : String) : EMap
    receiveMessage(pairIndex : int) : String
    transmitMessage(message : String, pairIndex : int) : boolean
    validateMessage(messageDict : EMap) : boolean
}

EntanglementManager --> QuantumComm : quantumComm
MessageProtocol --> JsonHelper : jsonHelper
QuantumComm --> JsonHelper : jsonHelper
@enduml
```

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_celestrion-nexus-core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system simulates a small quantum communication layer. It manages entangled-pair resources, performs simplified measurements, sends and receives messages through those pairs, and represents transmitted messages as JSON-like dictionaries. The model is not a physics engine; it provides deterministic application-level behavior around a randomized communication primitive.

## 2. Functional Requirement

  An entangled pair is represented as a two-by-two numeric state generated from random amplitudes. The communication layer has a maximum number of stored pairs. Creating a pair adds it to the available
  pool until that limit is reached; attempts beyond the limit must throw `IllegalStateException`.

  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
MessageProtocol::formatMessage
Summary:
Formats a protocol-level message as a JSON object string. The object always contains recipient,
message, and a generated ISO instant timestamp; if metadata is non-null, it is also included.
Algorithm:
1. Create a new EMap.
2. Put "recipient" -> recipient.
3. Put "message" -> message.
4. Generate timestamp = Instant.now().toString() and put "timestamp" -> timestamp.
5. If metadata is not null, put "metadata" -> metadata.
6. Return jsonHelper.serializeMap(map).
Related Features:
jsonHelper; uses JsonHelper::serializeMap.
Related Classes:
JsonHelper, EMap, EJavaObject
Input:
recipient : String - recipient identifier.
message : String - message content.
metadata : EMap - optional metadata dictionary; may be null.
Output:
String - JSON object string, e.g.,
{"recipient":"Alice","message":"Hello","timestamp":"2025-...","metadata":{...}}.
Pre-condition:
recipient and message are non-null; metadata, if non-null, is map-like.
Post-condition:
No state changes; the timestamp is generated at formatting time.

MessageProtocol::validateMessage
Summary:
Strictly validates a message dictionary. Returns true for valid messages; invalid messages cause
IllegalArgumentException rather than returning false.
Algorithm:
1. If messageDict is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
2. For each required key in ["recipient", "message", "timestamp"]:
   - If the key is absent, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   - If its value is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
3. If messageDict contains "metadata":
   - If the metadata value is not an EMap, throw IllegalArgumentException.
4. Return true.
Related Features:
None.
Related Classes:
EMap, EJavaObject
Input:
messageDict : EMap - message object to validate.
Output:
boolean - true if valid. Invalid input throws IllegalArgumentException.
Pre-condition:
None.
Post-condition:
No state changes.

MessageProtocol::getMetadata
Summary:
Reads a metadata value by key from a message dictionary. Returns null if the message has no metadata,
if metadata is not map-like, or if the requested key is absent.
Algorithm:
1. If messageDict is null, return null.
2. Let metadata = messageDict.get("metadata").
3. If metadata is null or not an EMap, return null.
4. Return metadata.get(key), which is null when the key is missing.
Related Features:
None.
Related Classes:
EMap, EJavaObject
Input:
messageDict : EMap - message JSON object.
key : String - metadata key to read.
Output:
EJavaObject - metadata value, or null if missing.
Pre-condition:
None; the operation is tolerant of null input and missing metadata.
Post-condition:
No state changes.

MessageProtocol::addMetadata
Summary:
Adds or updates a metadata entry in a message dictionary. If the message has no metadata field,
a new metadata map is created and inserted. If existing metadata is not map-like, it is treated
as needing replacement with a new map.
Algorithm:
1. If messageDict is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
2. Let metadata = messageDict.get("metadata").
3. If metadata is absent, null, or not an EMap, create a new EMap, set messageDict["metadata"] to it,
   and use that new map.
4. Put key -> value into the metadata map.
Related Features:
None; this operation only manipulates the supplied EMap.
Related Classes:
EMap, EJavaObject
Input:
key : String - metadata key.
messageDict : EMap - JSON object message.
value : EJavaObject - any JSON value.
Output:
void.
Pre-condition:
messageDict is non-null; key is non-null.
Post-condition:
The message dictionary has a metadata map containing the given key mapped to the provided value.
If metadata was absent or invalid, a new metadata map is created.

MessageProtocol::removeMetadata
Summary:
Removes a metadata key from a message dictionary. If the message has no metadata field or the key is
missing, the operation is harmless and makes no change.
Algorithm:
1. If messageDict is null, return no-op.
2. Let metadata = messageDict.get("metadata").
3. If metadata is null or not an EMap, return no-op.
4. Call metadata.remove(key).
Related Features:
None.
Related Classes:
EMap
Input:
messageDict : EMap - message JSON object.
key : String - metadata key to remove.
Output:
void.
Pre-condition:
None; the operation is tolerant of missing metadata and missing keys.
Post-condition:
If the metadata map existed and contained the key, the key is removed.
Otherwise no change occurs. The metadata map is retained even if it becomes empty.

MessageProtocol::parseMessage
Summary:
Parses a raw JSON string into a message dictionary. Rejects null input, syntactically invalid JSON,
and non-object JSON values by throwing IllegalArgumentException.
Algorithm:
1. If rawMessage is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
2. Call EMap result = jsonHelper.parseObject(rawMessage).
3. If jsonHelper.parseObject throws an exception, rethrow/wrap it as IllegalArgumentException.
4. If the parsed result is not an EMap or is not a JSON object, throw IllegalArgumentException.
5. Return the parsed EMap.
Related Features:
jsonHelper; uses JsonHelper::parseObject.
Related Classes:
JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
rawMessage : String - JSON text to parse.
Output:
EMap - the parsed JSON object.
Pre-condition:
None.
Post-condition:
No state changes; returns a newly parsed message map.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_celestrion-nexus-core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system simulates a small quantum communication layer. It manages entangled-pair resources, performs simplified measurements, sends and receives messages through those pairs, and represents transmitted messages as JSON-like dictionaries. The model is not a physics engine; it provides deterministic application-level behavior around a randomized communication primitive.

## 2. Functional Requirement

  An entangled pair is represented as a two-by-two numeric state generated from random amplitudes. The communication layer has a maximum number of stored pairs. Creating a pair adds it to the available
  pool until that limit is reached; attempts beyond the limit must throw `IllegalStateException`.

  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
MessageProtocol::formatMessage
Summary:
Formats a protocol-level message as a JSON object string. The object always contains recipient,
message, and a generated ISO instant timestamp; if metadata is non-null, it is also included.
Algorithm:
1. Create a new EMap.
2. Put "recipient" -> recipient.
3. Put "message" -> message.
4. Generate timestamp = Instant.now().toString() and put "timestamp" -> timestamp.
5. If metadata is not null, put "metadata" -> metadata.
6. Return jsonHelper.serializeMap(map).
Related Features:
jsonHelper; uses JsonHelper::serializeMap.
Related Classes:
JsonHelper, EMap, EJavaObject
Input:
recipient : String - recipient identifier.
message : String - message content.
metadata : EMap - optional metadata dictionary; may be null.
Output:
String - JSON object string, e.g.,
{"recipient":"Alice","message":"Hello","timestamp":"2025-...","metadata":{...}}.
Pre-condition:
recipient and message are non-null; metadata, if non-null, is map-like.
Post-condition:
No state changes; the timestamp is generated at formatting time.

MessageProtocol::validateMessage
Summary:
Strictly validates a message dictionary. Returns true for valid messages; invalid messages cause
IllegalArgumentException rather than returning false.
Algorithm:
1. If messageDict is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
2. For each required key in ["recipient", "message", "timestamp"]:
   - If the key is absent, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   - If its value is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
3. If messageDict contains "metadata":
   - If the metadata value is not an EMap, throw IllegalArgumentException.
4. Return true.
Related Features:
None.
Related Classes:
EMap, EJavaObject
Input:
messageDict : EMap - message object to validate.
Output:
boolean - true if valid. Invalid input throws IllegalArgumentException.
Pre-condition:
None.
Post-condition:
No state changes.

MessageProtocol::getMetadata
Summary:
Reads a metadata value by key from a message dictionary. Returns null if the message has no metadata,
if metadata is not map-like, or if the requested key is absent.
Algorithm:
1. If messageDict is null, return null.
2. Let metadata = messageDict.get("metadata").
3. If metadata is null or not an EMap, return null.
4. Return metadata.get(key), which is null when the key is missing.
Related Features:
None.
Related Classes:
EMap, EJavaObject
Input:
messageDict : EMap - message JSON object.
key : String - metadata key to read.
Output:
EJavaObject - metadata value, or null if missing.
Pre-condition:
None; the operation is tolerant of null input and missing metadata.
Post-condition:
No state changes.

3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_celestrion-nexus-core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system simulates a small quantum communication layer. It manages entangled-pair resources, performs simplified measurements, sends and receives messages through those pairs, and represents transmitted messages as JSON-like dictionaries. The model is not a physics engine; it provides deterministic application-level behavior around a randomized communication primitive.

## 2. Functional Requirement

  An entangled pair is represented as a two-by-two numeric state generated from random amplitudes. The communication layer has a maximum number of stored pairs. Creating a pair adds it to the available
  pool until that limit is reached; attempts beyond the limit must throw `IllegalStateException`.

  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title nexus Model

class EntanglementManager {
    activePairs : List<DoubleArray2D>
    quantumComm : QuantumComm
    establishConnection() : EIntegerObject
    listActivePairs() : void
    receiveMessage(pairIndex : int) : String
    removePair(pairIndex : int) : void
    sendMessage(message : String, pairIndex : int) : void
}
class JsonHelper {
    escapeString(s : String) : String
    parseArray(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : List<EJavaObject>
    parseBool(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : boolean
    parseNull(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : EJavaObject
    parseNum(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : Number
    parseObj(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : EMap
    parseObject(json : String) : EMap
    parseStr(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : List<String>
    parseValue(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : EJavaObject
    serializeMap(map : EMap) : String
    skipWhitespace(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : void
    toJsonString(s : String) : String
    toJsonValue(value : EJavaObject) : String
}
class MessageProtocol {
    jsonHelper : JsonHelper
    addMetadata(key : String, messageDict : EMap, value : EJavaObject) : void
    formatMessage(recipient : String, message : String, metadata : EMap) : String
    getMetadata(messageDict : EMap, key : String) : EJavaObject
    parseMessage(rawMessage : String) : EMap
    removeMetadata(messageDict : EMap, key : String) : void
    validateMessage(messageDict : EMap) : boolean
}
class QuantumComm {
    entangledPairs : List<DoubleArray2D>
    maxPairs : int
    random : Random
    jsonHelper : JsonHelper
    createEntangledPair() : DoubleArray2D
    formatMessage(recipient : String, message : String) : String
    measureState(pairIndex : int) : int
    parseMessage(rawMessage : String) : EMap
    receiveMessage(pairIndex : int) : String
    transmitMessage(message : String, pairIndex : int) : boolean
    validateMessage(messageDict : EMap) : boolean
}

EntanglementManager --> QuantumComm : quantumComm
MessageProtocol --> JsonHelper : jsonHelper
QuantumComm --> JsonHelper : jsonHelper
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
MessageProtocol::formatMessage
Summary:
Formats a protocol-level message as a JSON object string. The object always contains recipient,
message, and a generated ISO instant timestamp; if metadata is non-null, it is also included.
Algorithm:
1. Create a new EMap.
2. Put "recipient" -> recipient.
3. Put "message" -> message.
4. Generate timestamp = Instant.now().toString() and put "timestamp" -> timestamp.
5. If metadata is not null, put "metadata" -> metadata.
6. Return jsonHelper.serializeMap(map).
Related Features:
jsonHelper; uses JsonHelper::serializeMap.
Related Classes:
JsonHelper, EMap, EJavaObject
Input:
recipient : String - recipient identifier.
message : String - message content.
metadata : EMap - optional metadata dictionary; may be null.
Output:
String - JSON object string, e.g.,
{"recipient":"Alice","message":"Hello","timestamp":"2025-...","metadata":{...}}.
Pre-condition:
recipient and message are non-null; metadata, if non-null, is map-like.
Post-condition:
No state changes; the timestamp is generated at formatting time.

MessageProtocol::validateMessage
Summary:
Strictly validates a message dictionary. Returns true for valid messages; invalid messages cause
IllegalArgumentException rather than returning false.
Algorithm:
1. If messageDict is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
2. For each required key in ["recipient", "message", "timestamp"]:
   - If the key is absent, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   - If its value is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
3. If messageDict contains "metadata":
   - If the metadata value is not an EMap, throw IllegalArgumentException.
4. Return true.
Related Features:
None.
Related Classes:
EMap, EJavaObject
Input:
messageDict : EMap - message object to validate.
Output:
boolean - true if valid. Invalid input throws IllegalArgumentException.
Pre-condition:
None.
Post-condition:
No state changes.

MessageProtocol::getMetadata
Summary:
Reads a metadata value by key from a message dictionary. Returns null if the message has no metadata,
if metadata is not map-like, or if the requested key is absent.
Algorithm:
1. If messageDict is null, return null.
2. Let metadata = messageDict.get("metadata").
3. If metadata is null or not an EMap, return null.
4. Return metadata.get(key), which is null when the key is missing.
Related Features:
None.
Related Classes:
EMap, EJavaObject
Input:
messageDict : EMap - message JSON object.
key : String - metadata key to read.
Output:
EJavaObject - metadata value, or null if missing.
Pre-condition:
None; the operation is tolerant of null input and missing metadata.
Post-condition:
No state changes.

MessageProtocol::addMetadata
Summary:
Adds or updates a metadata entry in a message dictionary. If the message has no metadata field,
a new metadata map is created and inserted. If existing metadata is not map-like, it is treated
as needing replacement with a new map.
Algorithm:
1. If messageDict is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
2. Let metadata = messageDict.get("metadata").
3. If metadata is absent, null, or not an EMap, create a new EMap, set messageDict["metadata"] to it,
   and use that new map.
4. Put key -> value into the metadata map.
Related Features:
None; this operation only manipulates the supplied EMap.
Related Classes:
EMap, EJavaObject
Input:
key : String - metadata key.
messageDict : EMap - JSON object message.
value : EJavaObject - any JSON value.
Output:
void.
Pre-condition:
messageDict is non-null; key is non-null.
Post-condition:
The message dictionary has a metadata map containing the given key mapped to the provided value.
If metadata was absent or invalid, a new metadata map is created.

MessageProtocol::removeMetadata
Summary:
Removes a metadata key from a message dictionary. If the message has no metadata field or the key is
missing, the operation is harmless and makes no change.
Algorithm:
1. If messageDict is null, return no-op.
2. Let metadata = messageDict.get("metadata").
3. If metadata is null or not an EMap, return no-op.
4. Call metadata.remove(key).
Related Features:
None.
Related Classes:
EMap
Input:
messageDict : EMap - message JSON object.
key : String - metadata key to remove.
Output:
void.
Pre-condition:
None; the operation is tolerant of missing metadata and missing keys.
Post-condition:
If the metadata map existed and contained the key, the key is removed.
Otherwise no change occurs. The metadata map is retained even if it becomes empty.

MessageProtocol::parseMessage
Summary:
Parses a raw JSON string into a message dictionary. Rejects null input, syntactically invalid JSON,
and non-object JSON values by throwing IllegalArgumentException.
Algorithm:
1. If rawMessage is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
2. Call EMap result = jsonHelper.parseObject(rawMessage).
3. If jsonHelper.parseObject throws an exception, rethrow/wrap it as IllegalArgumentException.
4. If the parsed result is not an EMap or is not a JSON object, throw IllegalArgumentException.
5. Return the parsed EMap.
Related Features:
jsonHelper; uses JsonHelper::parseObject.
Related Classes:
JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
rawMessage : String - JSON text to parse.
Output:
EMap - the parsed JSON object.
Pre-condition:
None.
Post-condition:
No state changes; returns a newly parsed message map.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;

8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

# Operation Specifications
JsonHelper::skipWhitespace
Summary:
Advances the current parse position over JSON insignificant whitespace characters.
Algorithm:
1. While pos[0] < s.length() and Character.isWhitespace(s.charAt(pos[0])), increment pos[0].
2. Stop at the first non-whitespace character or at the end of the string.
Related Features:
None.
Related Classes:
None
Input:
s : String - JSON text.
pos : IntArrary - a length-one int array used as a mutable parse cursor;
      pos[0] must initially be a valid index in [0, s.length()].
Output:
void. The result is communicated by updating pos[0] to the first non-whitespace index or s.length().
Pre-condition:
s is non-null; pos is non-null and has length 1; 0 <= pos[0] <= s.length().
Post-condition:
pos[0] is advanced past leading whitespace; no other state changes occur.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_celestrion-nexus-core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system simulates a small quantum communication layer. It manages entangled-pair resources, performs simplified measurements, sends and receives messages through those pairs, and represents transmitted messages as JSON-like dictionaries. The model is not a physics engine; it provides deterministic application-level behavior around a randomized communication primitive.

## 2. Functional Requirement

  An entangled pair is represented as a two-by-two numeric state generated from random amplitudes. The communication layer has a maximum number of stored pairs. Creating a pair adds it to the available
  pool until that limit is reached; attempts beyond the limit must throw `IllegalStateException`.

  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
QuantumComm::measureState
Summary:
Measures the lower-level entangled pair at the requested pool index and returns a binary result, 0 or 1.
Invalid pair indexes are rejected with IndexOutOfBoundsException.
Algorithm:
1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= entangledPairs.size(), throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
2. Get DoubleArray2D state = entangledPairs.get(pairIndex).
3. Compute branch strengths:
   - s0 = Math.abs(state[0][0]) + Math.abs(state[0][1])
   - s1 = Math.abs(state[1][0]) + Math.abs(state[1][1])
4. Return 0 if s0 >= s1, otherwise 1.
Related Features:
entangledPairs.
Related Classes:
DoubleArray2D
Input:
pairIndex : int - zero-based index into entangledPairs.
Output:
int - binary measurement result, either 0 or 1.
Pre-condition:
entangledPairs is non-null; pairIndex is within the available range.
Post-condition:
No state changes; the pair is not consumed or modified.

QuantumComm::validateMessage
Summary:
Strictly validates a message dictionary. Returns true for valid messages; invalid messages cause
IllegalArgumentException rather than returning false.
Algorithm:
1. If messageDict is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
2. For each required key in ["recipient", "message", "timestamp"]:
   - If the key is absent, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   - If its value is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
3. If messageDict contains "metadata":
   - If the metadata value is not an EMap, throw IllegalArgumentException.
4. Return true.
Related Features:
None.
Related Classes:
EMap, EJavaObject
Input:
messageDict : EMap - message object to validate.
Output:
boolean - true if valid. Invalid input throws IllegalArgumentException.
Pre-condition:
None.
Post-condition:
No state changes.

QuantumComm::receiveMessage
Summary:
Receives a message from the lower-level pair at pairIndex. Valid pairs produce the human-readable message
"Message received with measurement: <value>"; invalid indexes throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
Algorithm:
1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= entangledPairs.size(), throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
2. int value = measureState(pairIndex).
3. Return "Message received with measurement: " + value.
Related Features:
entangledPairs; invokes measureState.
Related Classes:
DoubleArray2D
Input:
pairIndex : int - zero-based index into the lower-level pool.
Output:
String - exactly "Message received with measurement: <0|1>".
Pre-condition:
entangledPairs is non-null; pairIndex is valid.
Post-condition:
No state changes; the pair is not consumed.

QuantumComm::formatMessage
Summary:
Formats a basic message JSON string containing recipient, message, and timestamp.
The timestamp is generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style; no metadata is included.
Algorithm:
1. Create a new EMap.
2. Put "recipient" -> recipient.
3. Put "message" -> message.
4. Put "timestamp" -> Instant.now().toString().
5. Return jsonHelper.serializeMap(map).
Related Features:
jsonHelper; uses JsonHelper::serializeMap.
Related Classes:
JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
recipient : String - recipient identifier.
message : String - message content.
Output:
String - JSON object string containing recipient, message, and timestamp.
Pre-condition:
recipient and message are non-null.
Post-condition:
No state changes; timestamp is generated at call time.

QuantumComm::parseMessage
Summary:
Parses a raw JSON string into an EMap. Rejects null input, invalid JSON, and non-object JSON values
by throwing IllegalArgumentException.
Algorithm:
1. If rawMessage is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
2. Call EMap result = jsonHelper.parseObject(rawMessage).
3. If jsonHelper.parseObject throws an exception, wrap/rethrow it as IllegalArgumentException.
4. If result is null or not map-like, throw IllegalArgumentException.
5. Return result.
Related Features:
jsonHelper; uses JsonHelper::parseObject.
Related Classes:
JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
rawMessage : String - JSON text.
Output:
EMap - parsed JSON object.
Pre-condition:
None.
Post-condition:
No state changes.

QuantumComm::transmitMessage
Summary:
Attempts to transmit a formatted JSON message through the entangled pair at pairIndex.
It validates the pair index and message, then applies a small simulated random failure probability.
Returns true on successful transmission and false on simulated failure.
Algorithm:
1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= entangledPairs.size(), throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
2. If message is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
3. Parse and validate the message:
   - EMap parsed = parseMessage(message)
   - validateMessage(parsed)
   Any invalid-message condition throws IllegalArgumentException.
4. If random.nextDouble() < TRANSMISSION_FAILURE_PROBABILITY, where the inferred constant is 0.05,
   return false.
5. Return true.
Related Features:
entangledPairs, random, jsonHelper; invokes parseMessage and validateMessage.
Related Classes:
JsonHelper, EMap, DoubleArray2D, Random
Input:
message : String - valid JSON object string representing a message.
pairIndex : int - zero-based lower-level pool index.
Output:
boolean - true if transmission succeeds; false if the simulated random failure occurs.
Pre-condition:
entangledPairs is non-null; pairIndex is valid; message is a valid JSON object.
Post-condition:
No structural state change. If transmission fails, the message is not delivered.
If transmission succeeds, the operation is considered successful and the pair remains in the pool.

4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_celestrion-nexus-core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system simulates a small quantum communication layer. It manages entangled-pair resources, performs simplified measurements, sends and receives messages through those pairs, and represents transmitted messages as JSON-like dictionaries. The model is not a physics engine; it provides deterministic application-level behavior around a randomized communication primitive.

## 2. Functional Requirement

  An entangled pair is represented as a two-by-two numeric state generated from random amplitudes. The communication layer has a maximum number of stored pairs. Creating a pair adds it to the available
  pool until that limit is reached; attempts beyond the limit must throw `IllegalStateException`.

  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

class EntanglementManager {

    parseArray(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : List<EJavaObject>
    parseBool(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : boolean
    parseNull(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : EJavaObject
    parseNum(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : Number
    parseObj(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : EMap
    parseObject(json : String) : EMap
    parseStr(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : List<String>
    parseValue(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : EJavaObject
    serializeMap(map : EMap) : String
    skipWhitespace(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : void
    toJsonString(s : String) : String
    toJsonValue(value : EJavaObject) : String
}
class MessageProtocol {
    jsonHelper : JsonHelper
    addMetadata(key : String, messageDict : EMap, value : EJavaObject) : void
    formatMessage(recipient : String, message : String, metadata : EMap) : String
    getMetadata(messageDict : EMap, key : String) : EJavaObject
    parseMessage(rawMessage : String) : EMap
    removeMetadata(messageDict : EMap, key : String) : void
    validateMessage(messageDict : EMap) : boolean
}
class QuantumComm {
    entangledPairs : List<DoubleArray2D>
    maxPairs : int
    random : Random
    jsonHelper : JsonHelper
    createEntangledPair() : DoubleArray2D
    formatMessage(recipient : String, message : String) : String
    measureState(pairIndex : int) : int
    parseMessage(rawMessage : String) : EMap
    receiveMessage(pairIndex : int) : String
    transmitMessage(message : String, pairIndex : int) : boolean
    validateMessage(messageDict : EMap) : boolean
}

EntanglementManager --> QuantumComm : quantumComm
MessageProtocol --> JsonHelper : jsonHelper
QuantumComm --> JsonHelper : jsonHelper
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
QuantumComm::measureState
Summary:
Measures the lower-level entangled pair at the requested pool index and returns a binary result, 0 or 1.
Invalid pair indexes are rejected with IndexOutOfBoundsException.
Algorithm:
1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= entangledPairs.size(), throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
2. Get DoubleArray2D state = entangledPairs.get(pairIndex).
3. Compute branch strengths:
   - s0 = Math.abs(state[0][0]) + Math.abs(state[0][1])
   - s1 = Math.abs(state[1][0]) + Math.abs(state[1][1])
4. Return 0 if s0 >= s1, otherwise 1.
Related Features:
entangledPairs.
Related Classes:
DoubleArray2D
Input:
pairIndex : int - zero-based index into entangledPairs.
Output:
int - binary measurement result, either 0 or 1.
Pre-condition:
entangledPairs is non-null; pairIndex is within the available range.
Post-condition:
No state changes; the pair is not consumed or modified.

QuantumComm::validateMessage
Summary:
Strictly validates a message dictionary. Returns true for valid messages; invalid messages cause
IllegalArgumentException rather than returning false.
Algorithm:
1. If messageDict is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
2. For each required key in ["recipient", "message", "timestamp"]:
   - If the key is absent, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   - If its value is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
3. If messageDict contains "metadata":
   - If the metadata value is not an EMap, throw IllegalArgumentException.
4. Return true.
Related Features:
None.
Related Classes:
EMap, EJavaObject
Input:
messageDict : EMap - message object to validate.
Output:
boolean - true if valid. Invalid input throws IllegalArgumentException.
Pre-condition:
None.
Post-condition:
No state changes.

QuantumComm::receiveMessage
Summary:
Receives a message from the lower-level pair at pairIndex. Valid pairs produce the human-readable message
"Message received with measurement: <value>"; invalid indexes throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
Algorithm:
1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= entangledPairs.size(), throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
2. int value = measureState(pairIndex).
3. Return "Message received with measurement: " + value.
Related Features:
entangledPairs; invokes measureState.
Related Classes:
DoubleArray2D
Input:
pairIndex : int - zero-based index into the lower-level pool.
Output:
String - exactly "Message received with measurement: <0|1>".
Pre-condition:
entangledPairs is non-null; pairIndex is valid.
Post-condition:
No state changes; the pair is not consumed.

QuantumComm::formatMessage
Summary:
Formats a basic message JSON string containing recipient, message, and timestamp.
The timestamp is generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style; no metadata is included.
Algorithm:
1. Create a new EMap.
2. Put "recipient" -> recipient.
3. Put "message" -> message.
4. Put "timestamp" -> Instant.now().toString().
5. Return jsonHelper.serializeMap(map).
Related Features:
jsonHelper; uses JsonHelper::serializeMap.
Related Classes:
JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
recipient : String - recipient identifier.
message : String - message content.
Output:
String - JSON object string containing recipient, message, and timestamp.
Pre-condition:
recipient and message are non-null.
Post-condition:
No state changes; timestamp is generated at call time.

QuantumComm::parseMessage
Summary:
Parses a raw JSON string into an EMap. Rejects null input, invalid JSON, and non-object JSON values
by throwing IllegalArgumentException.
Algorithm:
1. If rawMessage is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
2. Call EMap result = jsonHelper.parseObject(rawMessage).
3. If jsonHelper.parseObject throws an exception, wrap/rethrow it as IllegalArgumentException.
4. If result is null or not map-like, throw IllegalArgumentException.
5. Return result.
Related Features:
jsonHelper; uses JsonHelper::parseObject.
Related Classes:
JsonHelper, EMap
Input:
rawMessage : String - JSON text.
Output:
EMap - parsed JSON object.
Pre-condition:
None.
Post-condition:
No state changes.

QuantumComm::transmitMessage
Summary:
Attempts to transmit a formatted JSON message through the entangled pair at pairIndex.
It validates the pair index and message, then applies a small simulated random failure probability.
Returns true on successful transmission and false on simulated failure.
Algorithm:
1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= entangledPairs.size(), throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
2. If message is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
3. Parse and validate the message:
   - EMap parsed = parseMessage(message)
   - validateMessage(parsed)
   Any invalid-message condition throws IllegalArgumentException.
4. If random.nextDouble() < 0.05, return false.
5. Return true.
Related Features:
entangledPairs, random; invokes parseMessage and validateMessage.
Related Classes:
EMap, DoubleArray2D, Random
Input:
message : String - valid JSON object string representing a message.
pairIndex : int - zero-based lower-level pool index.
Output:
boolean - true if transmission succeeds; false if the simulated random failure occurs.
Pre-condition:
entangledPairs is non-null; pairIndex is valid; message is a valid JSON object.
Post-condition:
No structural state change. If transmission fails, the message is not delivered.
If transmission succeeds, the operation is considered successful and the pair remains in the pool.

leaving the active connection list unchanged.
Algorithm:
1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= activePairs.size(), return without action.

EntanglementManager::receiveMessage
Summary:
Receives a message through an active connection. If the active index is invalid, or if the lower-level
communication cannot complete, returns null instead of throwing. Otherwise returns the human-readable
message produced by QuantumComm.
Algorithm:
1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= activePairs.size(), return null.
2. Retrieve pair = activePairs.get(pairIndex).
3. Find lower-level index: commIndex = quantumComm.entangledPairs.indexOf(pair).
4. If commIndex < 0, return null.
5. Call quantumComm.receiveMessage(commIndex).
6. If the call throws IndexOutOfBoundsException or IllegalStateException, catch it and return null.
7. Return the resulting string.
Related Features:
activePairs, quantumComm, and quantumComm.entangledPairs for mapping the active pair to the lower-level pool.
Related Classes:
QuantumComm, DoubleArray2D
Input:
pairIndex : int - zero-based active-connection index.
Output:
String - "Message received with measurement: <value>" on success; null for invalid active index or communication error.
Pre-condition:
activePairs is non-null; quantumComm is initialized.
Post-condition:
No state is changed; a successfully received pair remains available.

EntanglementManager::establishConnection
Summary:
Creates a new lower-level entangled pair through QuantumComm and records it as an active connection.
Returns the new active-connection index on success, or null if the lower-level pair capacity has been reached.
Algorithm:
1. Invoke quantumComm.createEntangledPair() to create and store a new pair in the lower-level pool.
2. If creation succeeds, add the returned DoubleArray2D to activePairs.
3. Return activePairs.size() - 1 as an EIntegerObject.
4. If createEntangledPair() throws IllegalStateException because maxPairs has been reached,
   catch it and return null; no active entry is added.
Related Features:
activePairs, quantumComm, and the creation operation QuantumComm::createEntangledPair.
Related Classes:
QuantumComm, DoubleArray2D
Input:
None.
Output:
EIntegerObject - zero-based active-pair index on success; null if lower-level pair capacity is reached.
Pre-condition:
activePairs is non-null; quantumComm is initialized; maxPairs is a positive configured constant.
Post-condition:
On success, activePairs has one additional entry and the lower-level entangledPairs pool has one additional pair.
On capacity failure, activePairs and the lower-level pool are unchanged.

EntanglementManager::sendMessage
Summary:
Sends a formatted message through an active connection. Invalid active indexes are no-ops.
Simulated lower-level transmission failures are also ignored; the method returns void.
Algorithm:
1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= activePairs.size(), return without action.
2. Retrieve pair = activePairs.get(pairIndex).
3. Find lower-level index: commIndex = quantumComm.entangledPairs.indexOf(pair).
4. If commIndex < 0, return without action.
5. Call boolean ok = quantumComm.transmitMessage(message, commIndex).
6. If transmitMessage returns false, return without action.
7. If transmitMessage throws IndexOutOfBoundsException or IllegalStateException, catch and return.
   IllegalArgumentException for an invalid message is allowed to propagate.
Related Features:
activePairs, quantumComm, and quantumComm.entangledPairs for mapping the active pair to the lower-level pool.
Related Classes:
QuantumComm, DoubleArray2D
Input:
message : String - formatted JSON message string to transmit.
pairIndex : int - zero-based active-connection index.
Output:
void.
Pre-condition:
activePairs is non-null; quantumComm is initialized.
If transmission is attempted, message should be a valid JSON object expected by QuantumComm::transmitMessage.
Post-condition:
If the active index is valid and transmission succeeds, lower-level transmission is considered successful.
If the active index is invalid or transmission fails, no observable side effect occurs.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_celestrion-nexus-core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system simulates a small quantum communication layer. It manages entangled-pair resources, performs simplified measurements, sends and receives messages through those pairs, and represents transmitted messages as JSON-like dictionaries. The model is not a physics engine; it provides deterministic application-level behavior around a randomized communication primitive.

## 2. Functional Requirement

  An entangled pair is represented as a two-by-two numeric state generated from random amplitudes. The communication layer has a maximum number of stored pairs. Creating a pair adds it to the available
  pool until that limit is reached; attempts beyond the limit must throw `IllegalStateException`.

  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate helper class required outside the UML model.
```

  public Map<String, Object> parseMessage(final String rawMessage)
  {
    if (rawMessage == null)
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("Raw message must not be null");
    }
    Map<String, Object> result;
    try
    {
      result = getJsonHelper().parseObject(rawMessage);
    }
    catch (Exception e)
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("Invalid JSON", e);
    }
    if (result == null)

    }
    return result;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Strictly validates a message dictionary. Returns true for valid messages; invalid messages cause
   * IllegalArgumentException rather than returning false.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. If messageDict is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   * 2. For each required key in ["recipient", "message", "timestamp"]:

  public boolean validateMessage(final Map<String, Object> messageDict)
  {
    if (messageDict == null)
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("Message dictionary must not be null");
    }
    String[] requiredKeys = { "recipient", "message", "timestamp" };
    for (String key : requiredKeys)
    {
      if (!messageDict.containsKey(key))
      {
        throw new IllegalArgumentException("Missing required key: " + key);
      }
      if (messageDict.get(key) == null)
      {
        throw new IllegalArgumentException("Value for key is null: " + key);
      }
    }
    if (messageDict.containsKey("metadata"))
    {
      Object metadata = messageDict.get("metadata");
      if (!(metadata instanceof Map))
      {
        throw new IllegalArgumentException("Metadata must be a map");
      }
    }
    return true;
  }

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Creates a new lower-level entangled pair through QuantumComm and records it as an active connection.
   * Returns the new active-connection index on success, or null if the lower-level pair capacity has been reached.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. Invoke quantumComm.createEntangledPair() to create and store a new pair in the lower-level pool.
   * 2. If creation succeeds, add the returned DoubleArray2D to activePairs.
   * 3. Return activePairs.size() - 1 as an EIntegerObject.
   * 4. If createEntangledPair() throws IllegalStateException because maxPairs has been reached,
   *    catch it and return null; no active entry is added.
   * Related Features:
   * activePairs, quantumComm, and the creation operation QuantumComm::createEntangledPair.
   * Related Classes:
   * QuantumComm, DoubleArray2D
   * Input:
   * None.
   * Output:
   * EIntegerObject - zero-based active-pair index on success; null if lower-level pair capacity is reached.
   * Pre-condition:
   * activePairs is non-null; quantumComm is initialized; maxPairs is a positive configured constant.
   * Post-condition:
   * On success, activePairs has one additional entry and the lower-level entangledPairs pool has one additional pair.
   * On capacity failure, activePairs and the lower-level pool are unchanged.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public Integer establishConnection()
  {
    try
    {
      double[][] pair = getQuantumComm().createEntangledPair();
      getActivePairs().add(pair);
      return getActivePairs().size() - 1;
    }
    catch (IllegalStateException e)
    {
      return null;
    }
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Sends a formatted message through an active connection. Invalid active indexes are no-ops.
   * Simulated lower-level transmission failures are also ignored; the method returns void.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= activePairs.size(), return without action.
   * 2. Retrieve pair = activePairs.get(pairIndex).
   * 3. Find lower-level index: commIndex = quantumComm.entangledPairs.indexOf(pair).
   * 4. If commIndex < 0, return without action.
   * 5. Call boolean ok = quantumComm.transmitMessage(message, commIndex).
   * 6. If transmitMessage returns false, return without action.
   * 7. If transmitMessage throws IndexOutOfBoundsException or IllegalStateException, catch and return.
   *    IllegalArgumentException for an invalid message is allowed to propagate.
   * Related Features:
   * activePairs, quantumComm, and quantumComm.entangledPairs for mapping the active pair to the lower-level pool.
   * Related Classes:
   * QuantumComm, DoubleArray2D
   * Input:
   * message : String - formatted JSON message string to transmit.
   * pairIndex : int - zero-based active-connection index.
   * Output:
   * void.
   * Pre-condition:
   * activePairs is non-null; quantumComm is initialized.
   * If transmission is attempted, message should be a valid JSON object expected by QuantumComm::transmitMessage.
   * Post-condition:
   * If the active index is valid and transmission succeeds, lower-level transmission is considered successful.
   * If the active index is invalid or transmission fails, no observable side effect occurs.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void sendMessage(final String message, final int pairIndex)
  {
    if (pairIndex < 0 || pairIndex >= getActivePairs().size())
    {
      return;
    }
    double[][] pair = getActivePairs().get(pairIndex);
    int commIndex = getQuantumComm().getEntangledPairs().indexOf(pair);
    if (commIndex < 0)
    {
      return;
    }
    try
    {
      boolean ok = getQuantumComm().transmitMessage(message, commIndex);
      if (!ok)
      {
        return;
      }
    }
    catch (IndexOutOfBoundsException e)
    {
      return;
    }
    catch (IllegalStateException e)
    {
      return;
    }
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Receives a message through an active connection. If the active index is invalid, or if the lower-level
   * communication cannot complete, returns null instead of throwing. Otherwise returns the human-readable
   * message produced by QuantumComm.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= activePairs.size(), return null.
   * 2. Retrieve pair = activePairs.get(pairIndex).
   * 3. Find lower-level index: commIndex = quantumComm.entangledPairs.indexOf(pair).
   * 4. If commIndex < 0, return null.
   * 5. Call quantumComm.receiveMessage(commIndex).
   * 6. If the call throws IndexOutOfBoundsException or IllegalStateException, catch it and return null.
   * 7. Return the resulting string.
   * Related Features:
   * activePairs, quantumComm, and quantumComm.entangledPairs for mapping the active pair to the lower-level pool.
   * Related Classes:
   * QuantumComm, DoubleArray2D
   * Input:
   * pairIndex : int - zero-based active-connection index.
   * Output:
   * String - "Message received with measurement: <value>" on success; null for invalid active index or communication error.
   * Pre-condition:
   * activePairs is non-null; quantumComm is initialized.
   * Post-condition:
   * No state is changed; a successfully received pair remains available.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public String receiveMessage(final int pairIndex)
  {
    if (pairIndex < 0 || pairIndex >= getActivePairs().size())
    {
      return null;
    }
    double[][] pair = getActivePairs().get(pairIndex);
    int commIndex = getQuantumComm().getEntangledPairs().indexOf(pair);
    if (commIndex < 0)
    {
      return null;
    }
    try
    {
      return getQuantumComm().receiveMessage(commIndex);
    }
    catch (IndexOutOfBoundsException e)
    {
      return null;
    }
    catch (IllegalStateException e)
    {
      return null;
    }
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Provides a human-readable listing of all active pairs as a side effect. The method returns void,
   * so its purpose is monitoring/debugging output.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. Iterate over activePairs with index from 0 to activePairs.size() - 1.
   * 2. For each pair, output/record a line such as "Active pair <index>: <pair state>".
   * 3. If there are no active pairs, emit an empty listing or a header only.
   * Related Features:
   * activePairs.
   * Related Classes:
   * DoubleArray2D
   * Input:
   * None.
   * Output:
   * void. The output is a listing written to the configured output/log destination.
   * Pre-condition:
   * activePairs is non-null.
   * Post-condition:
   * No state changes are made.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT

  public void listActivePairs()
  {
    for (int i = 0; i < getActivePairs().size(); i++)
    {
      double[][] pair = getActivePairs().get(i);
      System.out.println("Active pair " + i + ": " + Arrays.deepToString(pair));
    }
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Removes an active connection from the manager's active-pair list. An invalid active index is ignored,
   * leaving the active connection list unchanged.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= activePairs.size(), return without action.
   * 2. Otherwise, remove activePairs.remove(pairIndex).
   * 3. Do not remove anything from quantumComm; the lower-level communication pool is independent.
   * Related Features:
   * activePairs.
   * Related Classes:
   * DoubleArray2D
   * Input:
   * pairIndex : int - zero-based active-connection index to remove.
   * Output:
   * void.
   * Pre-condition:
   * activePairs is non-null.
   * Post-condition:
   * If the index was valid, activePairs has one fewer entry. If invalid, activePairs is unchanged.
   * The lower-level pool is unchanged in either case.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void removePair(final int pairIndex)
  {
    if (pairIndex < 0 || pairIndex >= getActivePairs().size())
    {
      return;
    }
    getActivePairs().remove(pairIndex);
  }

                        break;
                    case 't':
                        sb.append('\t');
                        break;
                    case '/':
                        sb.append('/');
                        break;
                    default:
                        sb.append('\\');
                        sb.append(next);
                        break;
                }
            } else if (c == '"') {
                return sb.toString();
            } else {
                sb.append(c);
            }
        }

   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='manual'"
   * @generated
   */

  {
     pos[0]++;
    
        StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder();
    
        while (pos[0] < s.length()) {
            char c = s.charAt(pos[0]);
            pos[0]++;
    
            if (c == '\\' && pos[0] < s.length()) {
                char next = s.charAt(pos[0]);
                pos[0]++;
    
                switch (next) {
                    case '"':
                        sb.append('"');
                        break;
                    case '\\':
                        sb.append('\\');
                        break;
                    case 'n':
                        sb.append('\n');
                        break;
                    case 'r':
                        sb.append('\r');
                        break;
                    case 't':
                        sb.append('\t');
                        break;
                    case '/':
                        sb.append('/');
                        break;
                    default:
                        sb.append('\\');
                        sb.append(next);
                        break;
                }
            } else if (c == '"') {
                return sb.toString();
            } else {
                sb.append(c);
            }
        }
    
        throw new IllegalArgumentException(
            "Invalid message format."
        );
  }

- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.

You will be given a **Java Class** that is originally generated by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true` and later is completed by LLMs. Your task is to fix the **Compilation Errors** by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. Read the error messages carefully.
3. Do your best to fix the errors locally without introducing new types, fields, and methods.
4. You should not remove faulty methods to eliminate compilation errors.
5. Do not change parts without errors.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.
